Hanson swiftly condemned for labelling Indigenous Australians 'primitive race'
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-14
05:38:39 UTC
14:38:39 KST

Indigenous leaders condemn Pauline Hanson for 'primitive race' remarks on First Nations' Australians
In short:
Indigenous Australians Minister Malarndirri McCarthy has criticised One Nation leader Pauline Hanson for "derogatory" remarks made on a podcast last year.
Senator Hanson called Indigenous Australians "the most primitive race on Earth", remarks that have earned widespread condemnation by politicians.
What's next?
Senator McCarthy said it was up to the Senate to decide if Senator Hanson would face consequences for her remarks.
One Nation leader Pauline Hanson has drawn widespread criticism over resurfaced comments in which she called Indigenous Australians "the most primitive race on Earth".
Indigenous Australians Minister Malarndirri McCarthy has condemned Senator Hanson for "derogatory" and "completely racist" remarks, calling on her to apologise.
Asked last year on the conservative-leaning Jamie J podcast about schoolchildren learning Indigenous history in classrooms, Senator Hanson said it was a "complete waste of time".
She claimed that a teacher had contacted her about a change in curriculum, where instead of being taught about Australian history and civil rights, students were learning about Aboriginal culture and language.
"And I'll say it on your program, what the hell have they done in this country? You know, supposedly 60,000 years, they'd never invented the wheel," she said last September.
"There was nothing there. What are we actually holding up? A few, you know, rock paintings.
"Sorry they're the most primitive race on Earth and we're supposed to be holding them up and saying what they've achieved. They've achieved nothing."
Senator McCarthy called out the Queensland senator for the remarks, which she labelled divisive.
"It's totally outrageous, completely racist, but also divides our country in a way that we do not want to see happen right here in Australia," she told reporters in Canberra.
"I do believe that as all Australians, we have to stand up and stand out against such divisive commentary, no matter whether it's on a comic, cartoon show or whether it's on a podcast.
"That is not the Australia I know, it is not the Australia I want to see, and I call on all Australians to condemn such commentary."
Senator McCarthy said as a Yanyuwa Garrwa woman she was personally impacted by the comments and wanted to reassure First Nation Australians that the government stood with them.
"I'm here, I'm with you, and I totally reject the comments by Senator Hanson," she said.
She said Senator Hanson's constituents deserved "an absolute apology" and it was up to the Senate to decide whether she would face any consequences.
Indigenous leaders denounce 'false claims'
Liberal senator Kerrynne Liddle labelled the remarks "disgraceful".
"One Nation's platform of degrading Indigenous Australians is ignorant and does nothing to build our nation," she told the ABC.
"I do think the curriculum needs to reflect our whole history but Pauline Hanson went low to unfair, unnecessary and unacceptable insult."
Jacinta Nampijinpa Price, who Senator Hanson previously asked to stand for One Nation, also expressed her "disappointment".
"Some of the issues raised by Pauline Hanson are serious debates, many of which I have been very vocal about, but yet again, Senator Hanson has dragged the conversation into appalling territory," she said in a statement.
"It is a demonstration of wilful ignorance on her part, and reveals her complete failure to understand Australia's First Peoples."
Independent Victorian senator Lidia Thorpe echoed the sentiments, labelling them "false claims".
"We have generations, thousands of generations of knowledge of this country. We are the first scientists, agriculturalists; we maintained and sustained our land and our water and our totems for thousands of generations and we are still continuing to do that," she said.
"So that is completely false and it is unfortunate that Senator Hanson is putting up that narrative."
Indigenous author and human rights activist Thomas Mayo believes Australians do not share Senator Hanson's view that Indigenous Australians are "primitive".
"Many Australians have learnt that Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples built sophisticated systems of law, governance, culture, trade, and land management over tens of thousands of years," he said.
"We have survived invasion, dispossession and every attempt to erase us. We are still here and will always remain in the rich tapestry of Australian identity."
Labor senator Jana Stewart, the youngest Aboriginal woman to serve in federal parliament, said the rhetoric had been used by One Nation for decades and was reached for "every time it runs out of ideas".
"Our mob know exactly who we are, and One Nation does not get to define us,"
"There's nothing brave about turning Australians against each other. It's the most primitive politics there is, and every Australian should reject it."
Politicians condemn Hanson's comments
Shadow Indigenous Affairs Minister Julian Leeser said "belittling Indigenous culture attacks part of what makes Australia great" as he called on all elements of Australia to be celebrated.
"There are three great strands to the Australian story: we have an Indigenous heritage, a British foundation and a multicultural character," he said.
Nationals leader Matt Canavan disagreed with Senator Hanson's characterisation and was against "demonising" a whole group or part of Australia's history.
"It is wrong, divisive re-reading of our nation's history and ultimately it weakens our bonds as a country and reduces patriotism because you are rejecting part of your heritage," he told the ABC.
He said censuring Senator Hanson could make her "a martyr" and only further boost her in the polls.
Greens leader Larissa Waters said the comments were "vile" and whitewash 60,000 years of proud history.
"It just makes people full of hate and fear," she said.
"It's pretty clear that Pauline Hanson is finding it hard to find anyone she doesn't hate. She can't stand veterans. She's not happy about people on the NDIS. She doesn't like people using public housing and she doesn't like First Nations people."
Social Services Minister Tanya Plibersek said the unearthed comments show there is not a group in society the One Nation leader is not prepared to "kick down".
"I think it's only for someone who doesn't know anything about Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture who can call it primitive," Ms Plibersek told News24.
The minister called out Senator Hanson's lack of knowledge, pointing to Brewarrina Fish Traps, which are thousands of years old, as an example of early agricultural Indigenous stewardship.
"I bet she doesn't know about that. If you don't take the time to learn about a culture you've got no right to call it primitive," she said.

Ukraine's prosecutor general Kravchenko signs notice of suspicion against NABU head
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-14
08:35:00 EEST

Ukraine's prosecutor general Kravchenko signs notice of suspicion against NABU head
Prosecutor General Ruslan Kravchenko has signed a notice of suspicion against Semen Kryvonos, Head of the National Anti-Corruption Bureau of Ukraine (NABU), and accused NABU and the Specialised Anti-Corruption Prosecutor's Office (SAPO) of seizing materials from criminal proceedings against their heads during searches conducted as part of Operation Carthage.
Source: Kravchenko on a video address
Quote: "Operation Carthage was aimed not only at me. Its real purpose was to gain access to materials from the criminal proceedings concerning NABU and SAPO officials and employees and then remove the prosecutor general and halt the adoption of procedural decisions in these cases."
"As of today, the Prosecutor General's Office is providing procedural oversight in dozens of criminal proceedings investigating serious and particularly serious crimes committed by NABU and SAPO employees... Based on the evidence gathered, I have signed a notice of suspicion against NABU Head Semen Kryvonos."
Details: Kravchenko said that during searches at the Prosecutor General's Office's premises, NABU and SAPO officials gained access to legally protected case materials. Some documents and electronic data were allegedly copied and stolen.
In particular, Kravchenko suspects Kryvonos of falsifying documents to obtain unlawful benefits on a particularly large scale and arranging a sham adoption to create grounds for avoiding liability in court.
The second notice of suspicion concerns a person close to SAPO Head Oleksandr Klymenko. This person is accused of influencing judges of the High Anti-Corruption Court, offering them unlawful benefits and helping them evade military mobilisation. According to Kravchenko, these criminal proceedings were the reason for the "night-time raid" on the Prosecutor General's Office during the searches.
Meanwhile, Kravchenko commented on his decision to resign as Prosecutor General as follows: "This deliberate political decision does not constitute an admission of any wrongdoing, result from any deal or mean that I am giving up the ongoing fight to defend my reputation and integrity."
He said he would not allow his resignation to be used to halt all criminal proceedings, conceal or destroy evidence, or allow "those whose actions it has been investigating" to establish control over the prosecution service.
He also stated that a person close to SAPO Head Oleksandr Klymenko had been served with a notice of suspicion for influencing the head of the High Anti-Corruption Court, offering him an unlawful benefit and helping him evade military mobilisation.
Kravchenko also noted that a targeted information campaign is being waged against the Prosecutor General's Office.
Background:
- On 4 September, Ukrainska Pravda reported that NABU and SAPO were conducting searches at the premises of Prosecutor General Ruslan Kravchenko, his deputy Mariia Vdovychenko and Oleh Kiper, Head of Odesa Oblast Military Administration.
- On 5 September, the Prosecutor General's Office confirmed that NABU detectives had conducted searches at office premises "used by Kravchenko".
- Also on 5 September, NABU and SAPO announced that they had uncovered a criminal organisation which investigators said is run by the head of a department at the prosecutor general's office. Members of the organisation are suspected of regularly taking bribes from scam call centres and money laundering.
- On 7 September, amid the NABU and SAPO investigation into the "protection racket" involving call centres, Ruslan Kravchenko submitted his resignation as prosecutor general.
- As Ukrainska Pravda previously reported, representatives of the Prosecutor General's Office may have received approximately US$7,000 per month from each sham call centre.
This news has been updated since publication.

HCHR denounces Israeli attacks against Lebanese people
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-14
05:26:50 UTC
14:26:50 KST

In a statement released on Sunday, the human rights body lashed out at the international community’s silence over the continued Israeli atrocities in Lebanon, calling for taking urgent measures in this regard.
The Israeli attacks on Lebanon have resulted in the death and injury of dozens of civilians, including women and children, as a blatant violation of fundamental principles of human rights and international humanitarian law, the statement added.
Underlining that the recurrence of these crimes, coupled with a lack of effective accountability, signals the emergence of a dangerous cycle of impunity, the Council also warned against double standards in addressing human rights violations.
"Human rights are universal, and the value of a Lebanese civilian's life should not be deemed less than that of victims elsewhere in the world based on nationality, geography, or political considerations," the statement stressed, adding that "remaining silent in the face of the killing of women and children—while simultaneously claiming to uphold the universality of human rights—seriously undermines the credibility of international human rights mechanisms."
Highlighting the inaction of international human rights bodies, including the UN Security Council, the HCHR said that their "failure to take effective measures to halt these atrocities effectively emboldens the Zionist Regime to persist in its gross human rights violations and undermine the concept of international accountability."
It further called upon "the Human Rights Council, the Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights, and other international mechanisms to abandon their selective and political approaches," urging them to act for the immediate cessation of attacks on civilians, the protection of the right to life of the people of Lebanon, and the documentation and legal pursuit of the crimes committed.
"The right to life is a universal right, not a privilege contingent upon geography or politics," the HCHR statement concluded.
MNA

Ukraine loses almost 9,500 troops, mercenaries in past week — expert
Unknown
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-14
05:24:03 UTC
14:24:03 KST

LUGANSK, September 14. /TASS/. The Ukrainian army lost almost 9,500 troops, including foreign mercenaries, who were killed or wounded in the special military operation zone over the past week, Russian military expert Andrey Marochko said.
"As for the enemy’s medical and irrecoverable losses, about 9,480 Ukrainian militants and mercenaries were killed or wounded. Russia’s Battlegroup Center, operating in the Donetsk People’s Republic, inflicted the heaviest manpower losses on Ukrainian formations," he wrote on the VKontakte social media platform.
Russian forces destroyed 5,600 unmanned aerial vehicles, 12 unmanned boats, 19 seagoing vessels, 71 armored combat vehicles, 31 field artillery pieces, 25 electronic warfare systems and counterbattery radars, as well as nearly 600 combat vehicles of various types over the past week, Marochko added.

Battlefield sees over 200 combat clashes, most intense fighting in Pokrovsk front – Ukraine's General Staff
Ukraine
Unknown Publisher
2026-09-14
08:18:00 EEST
14:18:00 KST

Battlefield sees over 200 combat clashes, most intense fighting in Pokrovsk front – Ukraine's General Staff
A total of 208 combat clashes have occurred on the battlefield over the past day, with the most intense fighting continuing on the Pokrovsk and Kostiantynivka fronts, which saw a total of 46 Russian attacks.
Source: General Staff of the Armed Forces of Ukraine on Facebook, information as of 08:00 on 14 September
Details: On the North Slobozhanshchyna front and in the operational zone in Russia's Kursk Oblast, no Russian assaults were recorded. The Russians conducted 148 strikes on Ukrainian positions and settlements, including 10 with multiple-launch rocket systems. They also conducted four airstrikes, dropping 10 guided bombs.
On the South Slobozhanshchyna front, Ukrainian forces repelled six Russian attacks. The Russians attempted to advance towards the settlements of Kozacha Lopan, Hrafskе and Volokhivka and near Lyman and Starytsia.
On the Kupiansk front, the Russians attempted to improve their positions three times, conducting assaults towards the village of Kivsharivka.
On the Lyman front, Ukrainian forces repelled Russian attempts to break through their defences, with the Russians conducting three attacks near the settlements of Novoserhiivka and Novoselivka.
On the Sloviansk front, the Russians conducted eight assaults near the settlements of Yampil, Ozerne, Kryva Luka, Rai-Oleksandrivka and Mykolaivka.
On the Kramatorsk front, the Russians conducted one attack towards the village of Yurkivka.
On the Kostiantynivka front, 20 Russian attacks were recorded. The Russians conducted assaults near the settlements of Kostiantynivka, Dovha Balka, Rusyn Yar, Toretske and Kucheriv Yar and towards Mykolaipillia.
On the Pokrovsk front, the Russians conducted 26 attacks. They attempted to advance towards the settlements of Dobropillia, Myrne and Serhiivka and near Dorozhnie, Shevchenko, Udachne and Novomykolaivka.
On the Oleksandrivka front, the Russians conducted three assaults towards the village of Ternove.
On the Huliaipole front, the Russians conducted eight attacks on Ukrainian positions. They attempted to advance near the settlements of Hirke and Charivne and towards Rizdvianka.
On the Orikhiv front, the Russians attempted to improve their positions towards the village of Chervona Krynytsia.
On the Prydniprovske front, the Russians did not conduct any offensive actions.
On the Volyn and Polissia fronts, there is no evidence of any offensive Russian groups being formed in the area.
